I'm ready to share whatever might help you understand what I've been going through.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced any episodes where you heard voices, saw things that weren't there, or had thoughts that seemed real but weren't based in reality, especially during your periods of extreme mood swings?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. I haven't heard any strange voices or seen things that weren't actually there, and my thoughts have always felt grounded in reality.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your episodes of feeling extremely happy and energetic - have you ever had a period where you didn't need much sleep, spent excessively, or engaged in risky behaviors during these high-energy times?\n\nPatient: I definitely experience those times where my mood is incredibly high and I feel like I have endless energy to keep moving. However, I'm not really sure about those other things you mentioned. I don't think I've ever noticed needing less sleep, spending too much money, or doing anything risky during those periods.\n\nDoctor: During these high-energy periods, have you noticed any significant impairment in your social or occupational functioning, or have you needed to be hospitalized due to your symptoms?\n\nPatient: I have never been hospitalized for my symptoms. To be honest, I'm not quite sure if it has significantly affected my work or social life, as I haven't really thought much about it.\n\nDoctor: Can you estimate how often these high-energy periods occur and how long they typically last, and do you also experience periods of feeling down or depressed that seem to follow or precede these energetic episodes?\n\nPatient: I have been experiencing these high-energy feelings overall for about two years and three months, but I'm really not sure about specific patterns or how long individual phases last. Also, I don't really experience periods of feeling down or depressed at all.\n\nDoctor: Are you certain you don't experience any periods of feeling down or depressed, or could it be that you haven't noticed or acknowledged them, given that your high-energy periods are more prominent in your awareness?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am quite sure that I don't really experience periods of feeling down or depressed. I've never noticed any low periods, and I really haven't ever thought about myself having them.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to the times when you mentioned feeling irritable and angry over minor things - could those moments be related to or indicative of a low mood, even if you don't typically identify them as feeling 'down' or 'depressed'?\n\nPatient: No, my irritability and anger don't feel like a low mood or sadness at all. It feels more like an impatient, high-energy state where I am just driven to keep moving. My mood typically just shifts between feeling extremely happy and high, or easily annoyed and agitated.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble focusing on tasks, getting distracted easily, or acting on impulse without fully thinking things through, especially during your high-energy states?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure if I have trouble focusing or if I act impulsively during those times. However, I definitely experience having overflowing energy and constantly starting many different activities at once.\n\nDoctor: Can you elaborate on what you mean by 'constantly starting many different activities at once' - is this something that you feel compelled to do due to your high energy, or do you find it difficult to complete tasks once you've started them?"

Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
